# DataQ v0 — Implementation Plan

## Context

`plans/spec_v0` describes DataQ: make 1–10GB dataset analysis easy for non-technical users, via a pipeline of **pluggable** components (import → normalize → aggregate → extract → join → visualize), with every capability reachable by both a UI and an in-app AI agent.

The repo is currently empty apart from that spec. This plan sets up the project from scratch and builds the first vertical slice. The priority order the user set:

1. **The framework** — plugin contracts, registry, metadata layer, lineage. This is the actual deliverable.
2. **A few hours of work** — a side project. Scope must be a thin but *complete* vertical slice, not a half-built layer cake.
3. **Prototype on real datasets afterwards** — NYC taxi and cyber/auth logs first.

Decisions already made with the user:
- Backend Python (FastAPI + DuckDB), frontend React + TypeScript + Vite.
- Storage: Parquet-lake layout as the default, **but** pluggable so a single `.duckdb` file can hold everything (easier remote hosting).
- v0 scope = import + profile + query + charts **+ joins & aggregates**. The agent comes right after (Stage 6), but its contract is designed in from the start.
- First datasets: NYC taxi (geo/time/scale) and cyber-auth logs (IP, country, rarity annotation).

## Storage: DuckDB critique and the resulting design

DuckDB is the right engine here — vectorised, zero-ops, reads Parquet/CSV/JSON natively, handles 1–10GB on a laptop trivially, and pushes predicates into Parquet. Two real limitations shape the design:

- **Single-writer.** One process may hold a `.duckdb` file for writing. Fine for a single FastAPI process; it breaks the moment you scale out or run a background worker in a second process.
- **A `.duckdb` file is an opaque blob.** Checkpointing a dataset version means copying a table; you can't hand a version to another tool, and remote object storage isn't a natural fit.

So: **DuckDB is the query engine, not necessarily the storage format.** Split the two behind a protocol.

```python
class StorageBackend(Protocol):
    def write(self, ref: VersionRef, rel: duckdb.DuckDBPyRelation) -> StoredRef: ...
    def sql_source(self, ref: StoredRef) -> str:  # a SQL-referencable expression
        ...
    def drop(self, ref: StoredRef) -> None: ...
```

- `ParquetStorage(base_uri)` — **default for local.** `COPY (…) TO '{base}/{dataset}/{version}/data.parquet' (FORMAT PARQUET)`; `sql_source` → `read_parquet('…')`. Immutable versions = free checkpoints, and `base_uri` can become `s3://…` (via `httpfs`) with no other code change.
- `DuckDBTableStorage(db_path)` — **the easy-to-host option.** `CREATE TABLE "{dataset}__v{n}" AS …`; `sql_source` → the table name. Whole warehouse is one file: mount one volume on Railway/Fly, or scp it.

Selected by `DATAQ_STORAGE=parquet|duckdb`. Both are exercised by the same test suite.

**Catalog stays separate, in SQLite** (`catalog.sqlite`, via SQLAlchemy/SQLModel) in *both* modes. It takes many small transactional writes (jobs, steps, column metadata) — exactly DuckDB's weak spot, and putting it in the warehouse file would make the single-writer lock the bottleneck for the whole API. One extra small file is still trivially hostable.

## Architecture

### Core objects (`backend/src/dataq/core/`)

| Object | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `Dataset` | Logical table. `kind`: `source` / `derived` / `aggregate` / `join`. |
| `DatasetVersion` | Immutable checkpoint: schema, row count, `StoredRef`, producing step. |
| `ColumnMeta` | `name`, `physical_type`, `semantic_type`, `confidence`, `role` (dimension/measure/time/key), `stats` (null frac, NDV, min/max, top-k). |
| `Step` | One operation: `op`, `plugin_id`, `params`, `inputs[]`, `outputs[]`, status, logs. The lineage DAG; replayable. |
| `Job` | Async execution of one or more steps, with progress. |
| `SemanticType` | The metadata layer. Hierarchical (`geo.country_iso2` isa `categorical`), declares `joinable_with`, a normalizer, and a detector. |

`SemanticType` is what makes join suggestion, viz suggestion and normalization automatic rather than hand-wired. It gets its own registry.

### Plugin framework (`backend/src/dataq/plugins/`)

One `Registry`, one base class. Every plugin declares `id`, `version`, `title`, and a **Pydantic `params_model`**. That single model generates: the request body schema, the OpenAPI docs, the auto-rendered UI form, and the agent tool's JSON schema. This unification is the point — *one registry → REST → OpenAPI → agent tools → UI forms*.

Plugin kinds for v0:

1. **`Reader`** — `can_read(uri)`, `to_relation(conn, uri, params) -> DuckDBPyRelation`. Returns a *relation*, not Arrow batches, so 10GB imports stay inside DuckDB. Impls: `csv`, `parquet`, `ndjson`.
2. **`SemanticDetector`** — `detect(conn, rel, column, stats) -> list[Guess]`. Impls: `net.ip`, `geo.lat`/`geo.lng`, `geo.country_iso2`, `time.timestamp`, `identity.email`, `money.amount`, `categorical.enum` (low-NDV), `identity.key` (high-NDV).
3. **`Normalizer`** — preferably `sql_expr(col, params) -> str` so it pushes down into DuckDB. Impls: IP → canonical text + `UBIGINT`, timestamp parse/tz, country code upper/trim, numeric coercion.
4. **`Suggester`** — `suggest(ctx) -> list[Suggestion]`, where a `Suggestion` is `{title, rationale, action: {op, plugin_id, params}}`. **A suggestion is an executable action payload** — the UI renders it as a button, the agent calls it directly. Impls: `viz`, `aggregate` (time rollups, top-k, frequency tables), `join` (semantic-type match + sampled value overlap).
5. **`Visualization`** — `spec(dataset, params) -> VizSpec` = `{renderer, query: QuerySpec, spec: {...}, animate?: {field, steps}}`. `renderer` is `vega-lite` | `maplibre` | `table`. The frontend has a renderer registry keyed on that field — that's the frontend pluggability contract.
6. **`Extractor`** — row-level background enrichment (`extract(batch) -> columns`). v0 ships the interface + one regex impl; the LLM-backed one lands with the agent.

### Query layer (`backend/src/dataq/query/`)

`QuerySpec` — a structured, composable IR that compiles to DuckDB SQL:

```
{ source: DatasetRef | JoinRef,
  filters: [{col, op, value}], time_bucket: {col, interval}?,
  select: [{col, agg?, alias}], group_by: [], order_by: [], limit }
```

Both the UI filter builder and the agent emit `QuerySpec`. A raw-SQL escape hatch (`POST /api/query/sql`) runs on a **read-only DuckDB connection** with a statement-type check. Joins are materialized as `kind=join` datasets backed by a view, so downstream steps treat them like any other dataset.

### Jobs

`JobRunner` protocol; v0 impl is an in-process `ThreadPoolExecutor` writing job/step rows to the catalog, with `GET /api/jobs/{id}` polling plus an SSE stream. Swappable for RQ/Celery later without touching callers.

### API (`backend/src/dataq/api/`) — thin handlers over the registry

```
GET  /api/plugins                          registry introspection (id, kind, schema)
POST /api/sources/preview                  peek at a file before import
POST /api/datasets/import                  -> Job -> DatasetVersion
GET  /api/datasets  /{id}  /{id}/versions  /{id}/profile
POST /api/datasets/{id}/normalize          apply normalizers -> new version
POST /api/query        POST /api/query/sql
GET  /api/datasets/{id}/suggestions?kind=viz|aggregate|join
POST /api/aggregates                       materialize a rollup
POST /api/joins                            create a join dataset
POST /api/viz/render                       -> VizSpec + data
GET/POST /api/dashboards
GET  /api/jobs/{id}  (+ /stream)
```

Handlers must contain no business logic — they resolve a plugin and call `dataq.services.*`. The agent (Stage 6) binds `@beta_tool` functions to the **same service layer**, not to HTTP.

### Frontend (`frontend/`)

Vite + React + TS + TanStack Query + Tailwind. Types generated from the backend's OpenAPI via `openapi-typescript` (`make types`) so the contract can't silently drift.

Pages: **Datasets** → **Dataset detail** (profile, semantic-type chips, editable, suggestion buttons) → **Query** (filter builder + SQL toggle + result grid) → **Explore** (suggested charts) → **Dashboard** (saved grid of VizSpecs).

`renderers/index.ts` maps `VizSpec.renderer` → component: `react-vega` for `vega-lite`, `react-map-gl` + `deck.gl` for `maplibre`, a virtualized grid for `table`. Adding a renderer = one registry entry. Animation is a client-side timeline scrubber over `VizSpec.animate`.

## Project setup (reproducible + deployable)

```
dataq/
├── .mise.toml            python 3.12, node 22   (pinned; also .nvmrc, .python-version)
├── Makefile              setup | dev | test | lint | types | build | docker
├── Dockerfile            stage 1 node build -> stage 2 uv sync --frozen + serve
├── docker-compose.yml    app + /data volume
├── .env.example          DATAQ_STORAGE, DATAQ_DATA_DIR, ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
├── backend/
│   ├── pyproject.toml    uv; deps: fastapi, uvicorn, duckdb, pyarrow, pydantic v2,
│   │                     pydantic-settings, sqlmodel, anthropic; dev: pytest, ruff, mypy
│   ├── uv.lock
│   └── src/dataq/{core,plugins,storage,catalog,query,services,api,jobs}/
├── frontend/             package-lock.json committed
├── tests/                pytest; tiny fixtures under tests/data/
└── .github/workflows/ci.yml   ruff + mypy + pytest + npm run build
```

- **Python 3.12** deliberately, not the 3.14 on this machine — duckdb/pyarrow wheel availability is the constraint that matters, and `mise` will fetch it.
- **Node is not installed here**; `mise install` provisions it. `make setup` = `mise install && uv sync && npm ci`.
- Dev: `make dev` runs uvicorn on :8000 and Vite on :5173 with `/api` proxied.
- Prod: one container — FastAPI serves `/api` and the built static bundle at `/`. Config entirely via env (pydantic-settings), data under a single `/data` volume. Deployable to Railway/Fly with a volume and zero extra config.

## Build order

Each stage ends in something runnable.

| Stage | Content | Done when |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | Repo scaffolding, mise/uv/npm, Makefile, Dockerfile, CI, health endpoint, empty React app | `make dev` serves both; `make docker` builds |
| 1 | `core` models, catalog (SQLite), `StorageBackend` + both impls, `Registry`, `SemanticType` registry | Round-trip test: write a relation, read it back, in both storage modes |
| 2 | `Reader` plugins, import job, profiler + `SemanticDetector` plugins, normalizers | Import taxi + auth-log samples; profile shows correct semantic types |
| 3 | `QuerySpec` → SQL compiler, `/api/query`, read-only SQL path | Query endpoint returns correct rows; SQL injection/write attempts rejected |
| 4 | `VizSuggester`, `Visualization` plugins, `/api/viz/render`; frontend renderer registry | Charts + a taxi pickup map render in the browser from suggestions alone |
| 5 | `AggregateSuggester` + materialization, `JoinSuggester` + join datasets | Auth-log rarity table built as an aggregate, then joined back to annotate events |
| 6 | Agent: `@beta_tool` wrappers over the service layer, `client.beta.messages.tool_runner`, chat panel | "Show me the busiest pickup zones on a Thursday night" produces a chart |

**Stage 4 is the first genuinely demoable point.** Realistically stages 0–4 are the "few hours"; 5 and 6 are follow-on sessions. I'll build in this order and stop wherever the time lands rather than leaving a layer half-done.

Agent details for Stage 6 (already checked against current API guidance): Python `anthropic` SDK, model `claude-opus-5`, `thinking: {type: "adaptive"}`, streaming, `@beta_tool` + `client.beta.messages.tool_runner`. Tools = `list_datasets`, `profile_dataset`, `run_query`, `get_suggestions`, `create_aggregate`, `create_join`, `render_viz`, `save_dashboard`.

## Verification

- `make test` — pytest. Every plugin gets a golden test against a tiny fixture; the storage round-trip runs parameterized over both backends.
- **End-to-end smoke test** (`tests/test_e2e.py`, runs in CI): import a 1k-row taxi sample → assert `pickup_latitude` detected as `geo.lat` and `tpep_pickup_datetime` as `time.timestamp` → run a `QuerySpec` group-by → assert a map VizSpec is suggested → materialize an aggregate → join it back. Same for a synthetic auth-log fixture (`src_ip` → `net.ip`, country rarity aggregate).
- `make lint` — ruff + mypy on `backend/`, `tsc --noEmit` on `frontend/`.
- **Manual**: `make dev`, import the real NYC taxi Parquet (~GB), confirm the import job streams progress, profiling completes, and the map renders. Then re-run with `DATAQ_STORAGE=duckdb` and confirm identical behaviour.
- **Deploy check**: `make docker && docker run -p 8000:8000 -v ./data:/data dataq` — full app on `localhost:8000` from a clean container.

Assistant2026-08-23T17:38:36.051Z

Fair — the plugin layer was hand-waved. Let me work it out properly.

The core design decision: plugins are heterogeneous in contract but homogeneous in execution. Every plugin declares a kind (what it consumes/produces) and independently an execution mode (how the runtime must run it). Normalization and extraction turn out to be the same interface at different modes; visualization is different because it's inspect-mode and returns a document rather than data.

# DataQ v0 — Implementation Plan

## Context

`plans/spec_v0` describes DataQ: make 1–10GB dataset analysis easy for non-technical users, via a pipeline of **pluggable** components (import → normalize → aggregate → extract → join → visualize), with every capability reachable by both a UI and an in-app AI agent.

The repo is currently empty apart from that spec. This plan sets up the project from scratch and builds the first vertical slice. Priority order the user set:

1. **The framework** — plugin contracts, registry, execution model, metadata layer, lineage. This is the actual deliverable.
2. **A few hours of work** — a side project. A thin but *complete* vertical slice, not a half-built layer cake.
3. **Prototype on real datasets afterwards** — NYC taxi and cyber/auth logs first.

Decisions made with the user:
- Backend Python (FastAPI + DuckDB), frontend React + TypeScript + Vite.
- Storage: Parquet-lake layout by default, **but** pluggable so a single `.duckdb` file can hold everything (easier remote hosting).
- v0 scope = import + profile + query + charts **+ joins & aggregates**. The agent follows (Stage 6), but its contract is designed in from the start.
- First datasets: NYC taxi (geo/time/scale) and cyber-auth logs (IP, country, rarity annotation).

---

## Storage: DuckDB critique and the resulting design

DuckDB is the right engine — vectorised, zero-ops, reads Parquet/CSV/JSON natively, handles 1–10GB on a laptop trivially, pushes predicates into Parquet. Two limitations shape the design:

- **Single-writer.** One process may hold a `.duckdb` file for writing. Fine for one FastAPI process; it breaks the moment a background worker runs in a second process.
- **A `.duckdb` file is an opaque blob.** Checkpointing a version means copying a table; you can't hand a version to another tool, and object storage isn't a natural fit.

So **DuckDB is the query engine, not necessarily the storage format.** Split the two:

```python
class StorageBackend(Protocol):
    def write(self, ref: VersionRef, rel: duckdb.DuckDBPyRelation) -> StoredRef: ...
    def write_part(self, ref: VersionRef, n: int, batches) -> PartRef: ...   # for resumable jobs
    def sql_source(self, ref: StoredRef) -> str: ...   # a SQL-referencable expression
    def drop(self, ref: StoredRef) -> None: ...
```

- `ParquetStorage(base_uri)` — **default.** `COPY (…) TO '{base}/{dataset}/{version}/part-*.parquet'`; `sql_source` → `read_parquet('…/*.parquet')`. Immutable versions = free checkpoints, part files = resumable jobs, and `base_uri` can become `s3://…` (via `httpfs`) with no other code change.
- `DuckDBTableStorage(db_path)` — **the easy-to-host option.** `CREATE TABLE "{dataset}__v{n}" AS …`; `sql_source` → the table name. Whole warehouse is one file: mount one volume on Railway/Fly, or scp it.

Selected by `DATAQ_STORAGE=parquet|duckdb`; the same test suite runs against both. **Known constraint to document:** out-of-process job workers require `parquet` mode, because `duckdb` mode holds a single write lock.

**Catalog stays separate, in SQLite** (`catalog.sqlite`, SQLModel) in both modes. It takes many small transactional writes (jobs, steps, progress heartbeats, column metadata) — exactly DuckDB's weak spot, and putting it in the warehouse file would make the write lock the bottleneck for the whole API.

---

## The plugin layer

### The central idea

Plugins are **heterogeneous in contract, homogeneous in execution.**

Every plugin declares two independent things:

- **`kind`** — *what it consumes and produces*. This determines its Python interface. A visualizer and an extractor are genuinely different, so they get different interfaces.
- **`mode`** — *how the runtime must execute it*. This determines whether it runs in the request thread or as a checkpointed background job, and what facilities it's handed.

Separating these is what keeps the framework small. The stage a plugin belongs to (import/normalize/extract/aggregate) does **not** determine how it runs — an extractor can be a cheap regex (`pushdown`) or an LLM call (`external`), and the runtime handles both without the plugin knowing the difference.

### Execution modes

| Mode | Runs | Facilities the runtime provides | Typical latency |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pushdown` | One DuckDB SQL statement | Query construction, progress callback | seconds–minutes |
| `batch` | Job worker, streams Arrow batches | Batch iteration, part-file checkpointing, progress | minutes |
| `external` | Job worker, async pool | Concurrency limit, retry/backoff, **result cache**, cost accounting, budget cap, structured output, row-level failure isolation | minutes–hours |
| `inspect` | Request thread, synchronous | Read-only catalog + sampled query access | milliseconds |

`inspect` is the answer to "visualization is very different": visualizers and suggesters don't produce data at all — they produce a **document** (a spec, a list of suggestions). They're synchronous, cheap, and never create a job. Everything that produces data goes through the job path.

### The six kinds

```python
class Plugin(Protocol):
    id: str                    # "normalize.ip", "extract.llm_entities"
    kind: PluginKind
    mode: ExecMode
    version: str               # participates in cache keys
    title: str
    Params: type[BaseModel]    # ONE pydantic model -> request schema + OpenAPI
                               #   + auto-rendered UI form + agent tool schema
    accepts: Requirements      # e.g. semantic_types=["net.ip"], min_rows=...
    produces: Produces         # columns / semantic types / dataset kind added
```

`accepts`/`produces` is what makes plugins **discoverable**: given a dataset's column semantic types, the registry answers *"what can I do with this?"* — driving both the UI's action list and the agent's choices.

| # | Kind | Interface | Usual mode |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `Reader` | `to_relation(conn, uri, params) -> DuckDBPyRelation` | pushdown |
| 2 | `Detector` | `detect(col, stats, sample) -> list[SemanticGuess]` | inspect |
| 3 | **`Transform`** | **DatasetVersion → DatasetVersion** | any |
| 4 | `Aggregator` | `plan(inputs, params) -> QuerySpec` → new aggregate Dataset | pushdown |
| 5 | `Suggester` | `suggest(ctx) -> list[Suggestion]` | inspect |
| 6 | `Visualizer` | `spec(dataset, params) -> VizSpec` | inspect |

### `Transform` — one interface for normalize, extract, and annotate

This is the direct answer to *"is there a standard interface for normalization vs extraction?"* — **yes, they are the same kind.** Both take a dataset version and return a new version with columns added or rewritten. They differ only in `mode`, which selects which of three methods the plugin implements:

```python
# mode = "pushdown"  — normalization, regex extraction, type coercion
def sql(self, ctx: TransformCtx) -> SqlPlan:
    # returns projected/added SQL expressions; DuckDB does all the work
    return SqlPlan(add={"src_ip_canon": f"lower(trim({ctx.col('src_ip')}))"})

# mode = "batch"     — needs a Python library (geoip lookup, custom parsing)
def process(self, batch: pa.RecordBatch) -> pa.RecordBatch: ...

# mode = "external"  — LLM / network calls
async def process_rows(self, rows: list[dict], ctx: ExternalCtx) -> list[dict]: ...
```

The runner dispatches on mode; the *output contract is identical* in all three cases (a new `DatasetVersion` + a `Step` recording lineage). Everything downstream — versioning, profiling, the UI, the agent — is unaware of which mode was used.

`Aggregator` is a separate kind because it changes cardinality: it produces a **new Dataset**, not a new version of an existing one. It is expressed as a `QuerySpec` generator, which makes it `pushdown` by construction and keeps it composable with the query layer.

### Long-running jobs

Every non-`inspect` invocation becomes a `Job` containing one or more `Step`s. Dispatch by mode:

- **`pushdown`** — build one statement, `COPY (…) TO parquet` or `CREATE TABLE AS`. Progress via DuckDB's progress callback.
- **`batch`** — stream `rel.record_batch(100_000)` → plugin → writer. **Checkpoint every N batches** by flushing a part file and recording `rows_committed` on the step. Resume = skip committed parts.
- **`external`** — same streaming, plus an asyncio pool bounded by `plugin.max_concurrency`, retry with backoff, and a persistent result cache. Checkpointing matters most here: these are the runs that take an hour and cost money.

Resumability is exactly why the Parquet-lake layout is the default: each committed part is an immutable file, so resume is "read the watermark, skip those parts, continue."

**Job state:** `queued → running → (paused) → succeeded | failed | cancelled`, carrying `progress{rows_done, rows_total, pct, eta}`, `logs[]`, and for `external` also `cost{calls, tokens_in, tokens_out, usd, cache_hits}`.

`JobRunner` is a protocol. v0 impl = in-process `ThreadPoolExecutor` plus an asyncio loop for `external`, writing job/step rows to the catalog. Swappable for RQ/Celery/Temporal without touching a single plugin.

### Triggering a specific plugin — one uniform endpoint

Yes, there is a single API for this, and its uniformity is what makes it agent-usable:

```
GET  /api/plugins?kind=&applicable_to={dataset_id}
     -> [{id, kind, mode, title, params_schema, accepts, produces, cost_class}]

POST /api/operations
     { op: "import"|"transform"|"aggregate"|"join",
       plugin_id: "extract.llm_entities",
       inputs: [{dataset_id, version?}],
       params: {...},                    # validated against plugin.Params
       output: {name?, materialize: bool},
       dry_run?: true, max_cost_usd?: 5.0 }
     -> 202 {job_id, step_id}

GET  /api/jobs/{id}          # status, progress, cost, logs
GET  /api/jobs/{id}/stream   # SSE
POST /api/jobs/{id}/cancel

POST /api/inspect            # synchronous twin for inspect-mode plugins
     {plugin_id, dataset_id, params} -> the document (VizSpec / suggestions / guesses)
```

`GET /api/plugins` tells you what can go in `plugin_id`; each plugin's `params_schema` tells you what goes in `params`. **The UI's dynamic form renderer and the agent's tool schemas are both generated from that one response** — there is no second place where plugin metadata is written down.

`dry_run: true` runs the plugin over a sample (default 1000 rows) and returns sample output plus an extrapolated cost/time estimate without creating a version. This is a first-class flow, not an afterthought — it's how a non-technical user avoids launching a $40 extraction by accident.

### LLM-powered plugins

A plugin backed by Claude is just `mode="external"`. Everything hard about it is provided by the runtime so plugin authors never hand-roll it:

1. **Injected client.** Plugins receive `ctx.model` — never construct their own client. Centralizes auth/model choice/retries and lets tests inject a fake.
2. **Concurrency & batching.** Plugin declares `batch_size` and `max_concurrency`; the runner enforces them. The plugin just writes `async def process_rows(rows, ctx)`.
3. **Caching — automatic and mandatory.** Key = `sha256(plugin_id, plugin_version, params, model_id, input_payload)`, stored in a DuckDB cache table. Re-running an extraction over a superset of rows costs only the new rows. This is the single biggest thing that makes LLM plugins practical.
4. **Structured output.** The plugin declares an output Pydantic model; the runner uses `output_config={"format": …}`, so results arrive guaranteed-shaped. No parsing in plugin code.
5. **Cost accounting & budget.** Every call is attributed to the step; `max_cost_usd` aborts cleanly with partial results committed.
6. **Prompt-cache friendliness.** Stable prefix (system + few-shot) carries `cache_control`; volatile row data goes last; many rows per call.
7. **Row-level failure isolation.** A row that fails after retries yields `NULL` plus an `_error` column instead of killing the job. `stop_reason == "refusal"` is a row-level outcome, not a crash.
8. **Batch API opt-in.** A plugin may declare `supports_batch_api: true`; for large non-interactive runs the job uses `client.messages.batches` (50% cost) and polls.

**Agent-powered plugins.** A plugin whose implementation is itself a tool-using agent (e.g. a suggester that explores the data before proposing aggregates) is the same `external` mode, additionally handed `ctx.tools` — the **read-only** subset of the service layer (`run_query`, `profile_dataset`, `list_datasets`). Recursion guard: that tool set excludes `operations.create`, so a plugin can never spawn unbounded jobs. The top-level chat agent and an agent-powered plugin share one tool registry, differing only in permission scope.

API details already checked against current guidance: Python `anthropic` SDK, model `claude-opus-5`, `thinking: {type: "adaptive"}`, streaming, `@beta_tool` + `client.beta.messages.tool_runner`.

### Registration & third-party plugins

Built-ins register via decorator at import. Third-party plugins register via the `dataq.plugins` Python entry-point group, so `pip install dataq-plugin-geoip` adds capability with zero core changes.

### Frontend plugin registry (the mirror image)

Visualizers are `inspect`-mode: the backend returns a `VizSpec = {renderer, query: QuerySpec, spec: {...}, animate?: {field, steps}}`. The frontend has its **own** registry keyed on `renderer` → component (`vega-lite` → react-vega, `maplibre` → react-map-gl + deck.gl, `table` → virtualized grid). Consequence: a new chart type that reuses an existing renderer is a **backend-only** change; only a genuinely new rendering technology needs frontend code.

### v0 plugin inventory (driven by the taxi + auth-log datasets)

- Readers: `csv`, `parquet`, `ndjson`
- Detectors: `net.ip`, `geo.lat`/`geo.lng`, `geo.country_iso2`, `time.timestamp`, `identity.email`, `money.amount`, `categorical.enum` (low NDV), `identity.key` (high NDV)
- Transforms (`pushdown`): `normalize.ip`, `normalize.timestamp`, `normalize.country`, `normalize.numeric`
- Transform (`batch`): one example proving the non-SQL path
- Transform (`external`): one LLM extractor, stubbed behind the full `external` machinery so the path is real and tested with a fake client
- Aggregators: `agg.time_rollup`, `agg.topk`, `agg.frequency` (the rarity table)
- Suggesters: `suggest.viz`, `suggest.aggregate`, `suggest.join`
- Visualizers: `viz.histogram`, `viz.timeseries`, `viz.map_points`, `viz.table`

---

## Supporting layers

### Core objects (`backend/src/dataq/core/`)

| Object | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `Dataset` | Logical table. `kind`: `source`/`derived`/`aggregate`/`join`. |
| `DatasetVersion` | Immutable checkpoint: schema, row count, `StoredRef`, producing step. |
| `ColumnMeta` | `name`, `physical_type`, `semantic_type`, `confidence`, `role` (dimension/measure/time/key), `stats`. |
| `Step` | One operation: `op`, `plugin_id`, `params`, `inputs[]`, `outputs[]`, status, `rows_committed`, cost, logs. The replayable lineage DAG. |
| `Job` | Async execution of steps, with progress. |
| `SemanticType` | The metadata layer. Hierarchical (`geo.country_iso2` isa `categorical`), declares `joinable_with` + normalizer + detector. |

`SemanticType` is what makes join suggestion, viz suggestion and normalization automatic rather than hand-wired.

### Query layer (`backend/src/dataq/query/`)

`QuerySpec` — a structured IR compiled to DuckDB SQL:

```
{ source: DatasetRef | JoinRef,
  filters: [{col, op, value}], time_bucket: {col, interval}?,
  select: [{col, agg?, alias}], group_by: [], order_by: [], limit }
```

The UI filter builder, `Aggregator` plugins, `VizSpec`s, and the agent all emit `QuerySpec` — one IR, four producers. A raw-SQL escape hatch (`POST /api/query/sql`) runs on a **read-only** DuckDB connection with a statement-type check. Joins materialize as `kind=join` datasets backed by a view, so downstream steps treat them like any other dataset.

### Remaining API

```
POST /api/sources/preview            peek at a file before import
GET  /api/datasets  /{id}  /{id}/versions  /{id}/profile
POST /api/query      POST /api/query/sql
GET  /api/datasets/{id}/suggestions?kind=viz|aggregate|join
GET/POST /api/dashboards
POST /api/agent/chat                 (Stage 6)
```

Handlers contain no business logic — they resolve a plugin and call `dataq.services.*`. The agent binds `@beta_tool` functions to that **same service layer**, not to HTTP.

### Frontend (`frontend/`)

Vite + React + TS + TanStack Query + Tailwind. Types generated from OpenAPI via `openapi-typescript` (`make types`) so contracts can't drift. Pages: **Datasets** → **Dataset detail** (profile, semantic-type chips, editable, applicable-plugin actions, suggestion buttons) → **Query** → **Explore** (suggested charts) → **Dashboard**. A `<JobProgress>` component subscribes to the SSE stream and is reused everywhere. Animation is a client-side timeline scrubber over `VizSpec.animate`.

## Project setup (reproducible + deployable)

```
dataq/
├── .mise.toml            python 3.12, node 22   (also .nvmrc, .python-version)
├── Makefile              setup | dev | test | lint | types | build | docker
├── Dockerfile            stage 1 node build -> stage 2 uv sync --frozen + serve
├── docker-compose.yml    app + /data volume
├── .env.example          DATAQ_STORAGE, DATAQ_DATA_DIR, ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
├── backend/
│   ├── pyproject.toml    uv; fastapi, uvicorn, duckdb, pyarrow, pydantic v2,
│   │                     pydantic-settings, sqlmodel, anthropic; dev: pytest, ruff, mypy
│   ├── uv.lock
│   └── src/dataq/{core,plugins,storage,catalog,query,services,jobs,api}/
├── frontend/             package-lock.json committed
├── tests/                pytest; tiny fixtures under tests/data/
└── .github/workflows/ci.yml   ruff + mypy + pytest + npm run build
```

- **Python 3.12** deliberately, not the 3.14 on this machine — duckdb/pyarrow wheel availability is the binding constraint. `mise` fetches it.
- **Node is not installed here**; `mise install` provisions it. `make setup` = `mise install && uv sync && npm ci`.
- Dev: `make dev` → uvicorn :8000 + Vite :5173 with `/api` proxied.
- Prod: one container — FastAPI serves `/api` and the built bundle at `/`. All config via env (pydantic-settings), all state under a `/data` volume. Railway/Fly-deployable with a volume and no extra config.

---

## Build order

Each stage ends in something runnable.

| Stage | Content | Done when |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | Scaffolding: mise/uv/npm, Makefile, Dockerfile, CI, health endpoint, empty React app | `make dev` serves both; `make docker` builds |
| 1 | `core` models, SQLite catalog, `StorageBackend` + both impls, `Registry`, `SemanticType` registry | Write a relation, read it back, in both storage modes |
| 2 | `JobRunner` + Job/Step state machine + SSE; `Reader` plugins; import as a job | Import taxi + auth-log samples with live progress; cancel works |
| 3 | Profiler + `Detector` plugins; `Transform` kind with all three modes; `/api/operations` | Semantic types correct; a pushdown normalize and a batch transform both produce new versions; `external` path passes with a fake model client |
| 4 | `QuerySpec` compiler, `/api/query`, read-only SQL path | Correct rows; write attempts rejected |
| 5 | `Suggester` + `Visualizer` plugins, `/api/inspect`; frontend renderer registry | Charts + a taxi pickup map render from suggestions alone |
| 6 | `Aggregator` + join datasets | Auth-log rarity table built as an aggregate, then joined back to annotate events |
| 7 | Agent: `@beta_tool` wrappers over the service layer, `tool_runner`, chat panel; real LLM extractor plugin | "Busiest pickup zones on a Thursday night" produces a chart |

**Stage 5 is the first genuinely demoable point.** Realistically 0–5 is the "few hours"; 6 and 7 are follow-on sessions. I'll build in this order and stop wherever the time lands rather than leaving a layer half-done.

## Verification

- `make test` — pytest. Every plugin gets a golden test against a tiny fixture; the storage round-trip is parameterized over **both** backends.
- **Job machinery tests specifically**: kill a `batch` job mid-run and assert resume-from-watermark produces byte-identical output to an uninterrupted run; assert the `external` cache makes a second run over a superset issue calls only for new rows; assert `max_cost_usd` aborts with partial results committed. These are the parts most likely to be subtly wrong.
- **End-to-end smoke test** (`tests/test_e2e.py`, in CI): import a 1k-row taxi sample → assert `pickup_latitude`→`geo.lat`, `tpep_pickup_datetime`→`time.timestamp` → normalize → `QuerySpec` group-by → assert a map VizSpec is suggested → materialize an aggregate → join it back. Same for a synthetic auth-log fixture (`src_ip`→`net.ip`, country frequency aggregate joined back as a rarity annotation).
- `make lint` — ruff + mypy on `backend/`, `tsc --noEmit` on `frontend/`.
- **Manual**: `make dev`, import the real NYC taxi Parquet (~GB), confirm progress streams, profiling completes, map renders. Re-run with `DATAQ_STORAGE=duckdb` and confirm identical behaviour.
- **Deploy check**: `make docker && docker run -p 8000:8000 -v ./data:/data dataq` — full app on `localhost:8000` from a clean container.

Plan rewritten around the plugin layer. Direct answers to what you asked:

How plugins span very different stages — every plugin declares two independent things: a kind (its Python interface — what it consumes/produces) and a mode (how the runtime must run it). Six kinds, four modes, and they're orthogonal. Visualization is different from extraction because it's inspect-mode: it returns a document (a spec), synchronously, and never touches the job system. Everything that produces data goes through the job path regardless of stage.

Long-running jobs — pushdown becomes one DuckDB statement; batch streams Arrow batches and flushes a part file every N batches, recording a rows_committed watermark; external adds an async pool, retries, and a persistent result cache. Resume = skip committed parts. This is the concrete reason Parquet-lake is the default layout even though single-file DuckDB is easier to host.

API to trigger a specific plugin — yes, one uniform endpoint: POST /api/operations {op, plugin_id, inputs, params} → 202 {job_id}, with GET /api/plugins?applicable_to={dataset_id} enumerating what's legal and each plugin's params_schema defining the body. That single response also generates the UI forms and the agent's tool schemas — plugin metadata is written down in exactly one place. POST /api/inspect is the synchronous twin for viz/suggesters.

Standard interface for normalize vs. extract — they're the same kind (Transform: DatasetVersion → DatasetVersion), differing only by mode, which selects sql() vs process(batch) vs async process_rows(). Output contract is identical, so nothing downstream knows which was used. Aggregation is genuinely separate because it changes cardinality — it's a QuerySpec generator producing a new Dataset.

LLM-powered plugins — mode="external" plus eight runtime-provided facilities so authors don't hand-roll them: injected client, enforced concurrency/batching, automatic result caching (keyed on plugin version + params + model + input, so re-running over a superset only pays for new rows), declared structured output, per-step cost accounting with max_cost_usd, prompt-cache-friendly layout, row-level failure isolation, and opt-in Batch API. A plugin that is itself an agent gets ctx.tools scoped to the read-only service subset — it can't call operations.create, so it can't spawn unbounded jobs. There's also a dry_run flow that samples 1000 rows and extrapolates cost before a non-technical user commits to a $40 extraction.

I also split Stage 2 out so the job machinery lands before the plugins that depend on it, and added tests for the parts most likely to be subtly wrong (resume-from-watermark equivalence, cache hit behavior, budget abort).

Everything that produces data goes through the job path.\n\n### The six kinds\n\n```python\nclass Plugin(Protocol):\n    id: str                    # \"normalize.ip\", \"extract.llm_entities\"\n    kind: PluginKind\n    mode: ExecMode\n    version: str               # participates in cache keys\n    title: str\n    Params: type[BaseModel]    # ONE pydantic model -> request schema + OpenAPI\n                               #   + auto-rendered UI form + agent tool schema\n    accepts: Requirements      # e.g. semantic_types=[\"net.ip\"], min_rows=...\n    produces: Produces         # columns / semantic types / dataset kind added\n```\n\n`accepts`/`produces` is what makes plugins **discoverable**: given a dataset's column semantic types, the registry answers *\"what can I do with this?\"* — driving both the UI's action list and the agent's choices.\n\n| # | Kind | Interface | Usual mode |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | `Reader` | `to_relation(conn, uri, params) -> DuckDBPyRelation` | pushdown |\n| 2 | `Detector` | `detect(col, stats, sample) -> list[SemanticGuess]` | inspect |\n| 3 | **`Transform`** | **DatasetVersion → DatasetVersion** | any |\n| 4 | `Aggregator` | `plan(inputs, params) -> QuerySpec` → new aggregate Dataset | pushdown |\n| 5 | `Suggester` | `suggest(ctx) -> list[Suggestion]` | inspect |\n| 6 | `Visualizer` | `spec(dataset, params) -> VizSpec` | inspect |\n\n### `Transform` — one interface for normalize, extract, and annotate\n\nThis is the direct answer to *\"is there a standard interface for normalization vs extraction?\"* — **yes, they are the same kind.** Both take a dataset version and return a new version with columns added or rewritten. They differ only in `mode`, which selects which of three methods the plugin implements:\n\n```python\n# mode = \"pushdown\"  — normalization, regex extraction, type coercion\ndef sql(self, ctx: TransformCtx) -> SqlPlan:\n    # returns projected/added SQL expressions; DuckDB does all the work\n    return SqlPlan(add={\"src_ip_canon\": f\"lower(trim({ctx.col('src_ip')}))\"})\n\n# mode = \"batch\"     — needs a Python library (geoip lookup, custom parsing)\ndef process(self, batch: pa.RecordBatch) -> pa.RecordBatch: ...\n\n# mode = \"external\"  — LLM / network calls\nasync def process_rows(self, rows: list[dict], ctx: ExternalCtx) -> list[dict]: ...\n```\n\nThe runner dispatches on mode; the *output contract is identical* in all three cases (a new `DatasetVersion` + a `Step` recording lineage). Everything downstream — versioning, profiling, the UI, the agent — is unaware of which mode was used.\n\n`Aggregator` is a separate kind because it changes cardinality: it produces a **new Dataset**, not a new version of an existing one. It is expressed as a `QuerySpec` generator, which makes it `pushdown` by construction and keeps it composable with the query layer.\n\n### Long-running jobs\n\nEvery non-`inspect` invocation becomes a `Job` containing one or more `Step`s. Dispatch by mode:\n\n- **`pushdown`** — build one statement, `COPY (…) TO parquet` or `CREATE TABLE AS`. Progress via DuckDB's progress callback.\n- **`batch`** — stream `rel.record_batch(100_000)` → plugin → writer. **Checkpoint every N batches** by flushing a part file and recording `rows_committed` on the step. Resume = skip committed parts.\n- **`external`** — same streaming, plus an asyncio pool bounded by `plugin.max_concurrency`, retry with backoff, and a persistent result cache. Checkpointing matters most here: these are the runs that take an hour and cost money.\n\nResumability is exactly why the Parquet-lake layout is the default: each committed part is an immutable file, so resume is \"read the watermark, skip those parts, continue.\"\n\n**Job state:** `queued → running → (paused) → succeeded | failed | cancelled`, carrying `progress{rows_done, rows_total, pct, eta}`, `logs[]`, and for `external` also `cost{calls, tokens_in, tokens_out, usd, cache_hits}`.\n\n`JobRunner` is a protocol. v0 impl = in-process `ThreadPoolExecutor` plus an asyncio loop for `external`, writing job/step rows to the catalog. Swappable for RQ/Celery/Temporal without touching a single plugin.\n\n### Triggering a specific plugin — one uniform endpoint\n\nYes, there is a single API for this, and its uniformity is what makes it agent-usable:\n\n```\nGET  /api/plugins?kind=&applicable_to={dataset_id}\n     -> [{id, kind, mode, title, params_schema, accepts, produces, cost_class}]\n\nPOST /api/operations\n     { op: \"import\"|\"transform\"|\"aggregate\"|\"join\",\n       plugin_id: \"extract.llm_entities\",\n       inputs: [{dataset_id, version?}],\n       params: {...},                    # validated against plugin.Params\n       output: {name?, materialize: bool},\n       dry_run?: true, max_cost_usd?: 5.0 }\n     -> 202 {job_id, step_id}\n\nGET  /api/jobs/{id}          # status, progress, cost, logs\nGET  /api/jobs/{id}/stream   # SSE\nPOST /api/jobs/{id}/cancel\n\nPOST /api/inspect            # synchronous twin for inspect-mode plugins\n     {plugin_id, dataset_id, params} -> the document (VizSpec / suggestions / guesses)\n```\n\n`GET /api/plugins` tells you what can go in `plugin_id`; each plugin's `params_schema` tells you what goes in `params`. **The UI's dynamic form renderer and the agent's tool schemas are both generated from that one response** — there is no second place where plugin metadata is written down.\n\n`dry_run: true` runs the plugin over a sample (default 1000 rows) and returns sample output plus an extrapolated cost/time estimate without creating a version. This is a first-class flow, not an afterthought — it's how a non-technical user avoids launching a $40 extraction by accident.\n\n### LLM-powered plugins\n\nA plugin backed by Claude is just `mode=\"external\"`. Everything hard about it is provided by the runtime so plugin authors never hand-roll it:\n\n1. **Injected client.** Plugins receive `ctx.model` — never construct their own client. Centralizes auth/model choice/retries and lets tests inject a fake.\n2. **Concurrency & batching.** Plugin declares `batch_size` and `max_concurrency`; the runner enforces them. The plugin just writes `async def process_rows(rows, ctx)`.\n3. **Caching — automatic and mandatory.** Key = `sha256(plugin_id, plugin_version, params, model_id, input_payload)`, stored in a DuckDB cache table. Re-running an extraction over a superset of rows costs only the new rows. This is the single biggest thing that makes LLM plugins practical.\n4. **Structured output.** The plugin declares an output Pydantic model; the runner uses `output_config={\"format\": …}`, so results arrive guaranteed-shaped. No parsing in plugin code.\n5. **Cost accounting & budget.** Every call is attributed to the step; `max_cost_usd` aborts cleanly with partial results committed.\n6. **Prompt-cache friendliness.** Stable prefix (system + few-shot) carries `cache_control`; volatile row data goes last; many rows per call.\n7. **Row-level failure isolation.** A row that fails after retries yields `NULL` plus an `_error` column instead of killing the job. `stop_reason == \"refusal\"` is a row-level outcome, not a crash.\n8. **Batch API opt-in.** A plugin may declare `supports_batch_api: true`; for large non-interactive runs the job uses `client.messages.batches` (50% cost) and polls.\n\n**Agent-powered plugins.** A plugin whose implementation is itself a tool-using agent (e.g. a suggester that explores the data before proposing aggregates) is the same `external` mode, additionally handed `ctx.tools` — the **read-only** subset of the service layer (`run_query`, `profile_dataset`, `list_datasets`). Recursion guard: that tool set excludes `operations.create`, so a plugin can never spawn unbounded jobs. The top-level chat agent and an agent-powered plugin share one tool registry, differing only in permission scope.\n\nAPI details already checked against current guidance: Python `anthropic` SDK, model `claude-opus-5`, `thinking: {type: \"adaptive\"}`, streaming, `@beta_tool` + `client.beta.messages.tool_runner`.\n\n### Registration & third-party plugins\n\nBuilt-ins register via decorator at import. Third-party plugins register via the `dataq.plugins` Python entry-point group, so `pip install dataq-plugin-geoip` adds capability with zero core changes.\n\n### Frontend plugin registry (the mirror image)\n\nVisualizers are `inspect`-mode: the backend returns a `VizSpec = {renderer, query: QuerySpec, spec: {...}, animate?: {field, steps}}`. The frontend has its **own** registry keyed on `renderer` → component (`vega-lite` → react-vega, `maplibre` → react-map-gl + deck.gl, `table` → virtualized grid). Consequence: a new chart type that reuses an existing renderer is a **backend-only** change; only a genuinely new rendering technology needs frontend code.\n\n### v0 plugin inventory (driven by the taxi + auth-log datasets)\n\n- Readers: `csv`, `parquet`, `ndjson`\n- Detectors: `net.ip`, `geo.lat`/`geo.lng`, `geo.country_iso2`, `time.timestamp`, `identity.email`, `money.amount`, `categorical.enum` (low NDV), `identity.key` (high NDV)\n- Transforms (`pushdown`): `normalize.ip`, `normalize.timestamp`, `normalize.country`, `normalize.numeric`\n- Transform (`batch`): one example proving the non-SQL path\n- Transform (`external`): one LLM extractor, stubbed behind the full `external` machinery so the path is real and tested with a fake client\n- Aggregators: `agg.time_rollup`, `agg.topk`, `agg.frequency` (the rarity table)\n- Suggesters: `suggest.viz`, `suggest.aggregate`, `suggest.join`\n- Visualizers: `viz.histogram`, `viz.timeseries`, `viz.map_points`, `viz.table`\n\n---\n\n## Supporting layers\n\n### Core objects (`backend/src/dataq/core/`)\n\n| Object | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| `Dataset` | Logical table. `kind`: `source`/`derived`/`aggregate`/`join`. |\n| `DatasetVersion` | Immutable checkpoint: schema, row count, `StoredRef`, producing step. |\n| `ColumnMeta` | `name`, `physical_type`, `semantic_type`, `confidence`, `role` (dimension/measure/time/key), `stats`. |\n| `Step` | One operation: `op`, `plugin_id`, `params`, `inputs[]`, `outputs[]`, status, `rows_committed`, cost, logs. The replayable lineage DAG. |\n| `Job` | Async execution of steps, with progress. |\n| `SemanticType` | The metadata layer. Hierarchical (`geo.country_iso2` isa `categorical`), declares `joinable_with` + normalizer + detector. |\n\n`SemanticType` is what makes join suggestion, viz suggestion and normalization automatic rather than hand-wired.\n\n### Query layer (`backend/src/dataq/query/`)\n\n`QuerySpec` — a structured IR compiled to DuckDB SQL:\n\n```\n{ source: DatasetRef | JoinRef,\n  filters: [{col, op, value}], time_bucket: {col, interval}?,\n  select: [{col, agg?, alias}], group_by: [], order_by: [], limit }\n```\n\nThe UI filter builder, `Aggregator` plugins, `VizSpec`s, and the agent all emit `QuerySpec` — one IR, four producers. A raw-SQL escape hatch (`POST /api/query/sql`) runs on a **read-only** DuckDB connection with a statement-type check. Joins materialize as `kind=join` datasets backed by a view, so downstream steps treat them like any other dataset.\n\n### Remaining API\n\n```\nPOST /api/sources/preview            peek at a file before import\nGET  /api/datasets  /{id}  /{id}/versions  /{id}/profile\nPOST /api/query      POST /api/query/sql\nGET  /api/datasets/{id}/suggestions?kind=viz|aggregate|join\nGET/POST /api/dashboards\nPOST /api/agent/chat                 (Stage 6)\n```\n\nHandlers contain no business logic — they resolve a plugin and call `dataq.services.*`. The agent binds `@beta_tool` functions to that **same service layer**, not to HTTP.\n\n### Frontend (`frontend/`)\n\nVite + React + TS + TanStack Query + Tailwind. Types generated from OpenAPI via `openapi-typescript` (`make types`) so contracts can't drift. Pages: **Datasets** → **Dataset detail** (profile, semantic-type chips, editable, applicable-plugin actions, suggestion buttons) → **Query** → **Explore** (suggested charts) → **Dashboard**. A `<JobProgress>` component subscribes to the SSE stream and is reused everywhere. Animation is a client-side timeline scrubber over `VizSpec.animate`.\n\n---\n\n## Project setup (reproducible + deployable)\n\n```\ndataq/\n├── .mise.toml            python 3.12, node 22   (also .nvmrc, .python-version)\n├── Makefile              setup | dev | test | lint | types | build | docker\n├── Dockerfile            stage 1 node build -> stage 2 uv sync --frozen + serve\n├── docker-compose.yml    app + /data volume\n├── .env.example          DATAQ_STORAGE, DATAQ_DATA_DIR, ANTHROPIC_API_KEY\n├── backend/\n│   ├── pyproject.toml    uv; fastapi, uvicorn, duckdb, pyarrow, pydantic v2,\n│   │                     pydantic-settings, sqlmodel, anthropic; dev: pytest, ruff, mypy\n│   ├── uv.lock\n│   └── src/dataq/{core,plugins,storage,catalog,query,services,jobs,api}/\n├── frontend/             package-lock.json committed\n├── tests/                pytest; tiny fixtures under tests/data/\n└── .github/workflows/ci.yml   ruff + mypy + pytest + npm run build\n```\n\n- **Python 3.12** deliberately, not the 3.14 on this machine — duckdb/pyarrow wheel availability is the binding constraint. `mise` fetches it.\n- **Node is not installed here**; `mise install` provisions it. `make setup` = `mise install && uv sync && npm ci`.\n- Dev: `make dev` → uvicorn :8000 + Vite :5173 with `/api` proxied.\n- Prod: one container — FastAPI serves `/api` and the built bundle at `/`. ## Approved Plan:
# DataQ v0 — Implementation Plan

## Context

`plans/spec_v0` describes DataQ: make 1–10GB dataset analysis easy for non-technical users, via a pipeline of **pluggable** components (import → normalize → aggregate → extract → join → visualize), with every capability reachable by both a UI and an in-app AI agent.

The repo is currently empty apart from that spec. This plan sets up the project from scratch and builds the first vertical slice. Priority order the user set:

1. **The framework** — plugin contracts, registry, execution model, metadata layer, lineage. This is the actual deliverable.
2. **A few hours of work** — a side project. A thin but *complete* vertical slice, not a half-built layer cake.
3. **Prototype on real datasets afterwards** — NYC taxi and cyber/auth logs first.

Decisions made with the user:
- Backend Python (FastAPI + DuckDB), frontend React + TypeScript + Vite.
- Storage: Parquet-lake layout by default, **but** pluggable so a single `.duckdb` file can hold everything (easier remote hosting).
- v0 scope = import + profile + query + charts **+ joins & aggregates**. The agent follows (Stage 6), but its contract is designed in from the start.
- First datasets: NYC taxi (geo/time/scale) and cyber-auth logs (IP, country, rarity annotation).

---

## Storage: DuckDB critique and the resulting design

DuckDB is the right engine — vectorised, zero-ops, reads Parquet/CSV/JSON natively, handles 1–10GB on a laptop trivially, pushes predicates into Parquet. Two limitations shape the design:

- **Single-writer.** One process may hold a `.duckdb` file for writing. Fine for one FastAPI process; it breaks the moment a background worker runs in a second process.
- **A `.duckdb` file is an opaque blob.** Checkpointing a version means copying a table; you can't hand a version to another tool, and object storage isn't a natural fit.

So **DuckDB is the query engine, not necessarily the storage format.** Split the two:

```python
class StorageBackend(Protocol):
    def write(self, ref: VersionRef, rel: duckdb.DuckDBPyRelation) -> StoredRef: ...
    def write_part(self, ref: VersionRef, n: int, batches) -> PartRef: ...   # for resumable jobs
    def sql_source(self, ref: StoredRef) -> str: ...   # a SQL-referencable expression
    def drop(self, ref: StoredRef) -> None: ...
```

- `ParquetStorage(base_uri)` — **default.** `COPY (…) TO '{base}/{dataset}/{version}/part-*.parquet'`; `sql_source` → `read_parquet('…/*.parquet')`. Immutable versions = free checkpoints, part files = resumable jobs, and `base_uri` can become `s3://…` (via `httpfs`) with no other code change.
- `DuckDBTableStorage(db_path)` — **the easy-to-host option.** `CREATE TABLE "{dataset}__v{n}" AS …`; `sql_source` → the table name. Whole warehouse is one file: mount one volume on Railway/Fly, or scp it.

Selected by `DATAQ_STORAGE=parquet|duckdb`; the same test suite runs against both. **Known constraint to document:** out-of-process job workers require `parquet` mode, because `duckdb` mode holds a single write lock.

**Catalog stays separate, in SQLite** (`catalog.sqlite`, SQLModel) in both modes. It takes many small transactional writes (jobs, steps, progress heartbeats, column metadata) — exactly DuckDB's weak spot, and putting it in the warehouse file would make the write lock the bottleneck for the whole API.

---

## The plugin layer

### The central idea

Plugins are **heterogeneous in contract, homogeneous in execution.**

Every plugin declares two independent things:

- **`kind`** — *what it consumes and produces*. This determines its Python interface. A visualizer and an extractor are genuinely different, so they get different interfaces.
- **`mode`** — *how the runtime must execute it*. This determines whether it runs in the request thread or as a checkpointed background job, and what facilities it's handed.

Separating these is what keeps the framework small. The stage a plugin belongs to (import/normalize/extract/aggregate) does **not** determine how it runs — an extractor can be a cheap regex (`pushdown`) or an LLM call (`external`), and the runtime handles both without the plugin knowing the difference.

### Execution modes

| Mode | Runs | Facilities the runtime provides | Typical latency |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pushdown` | One DuckDB SQL statement | Query construction, progress callback | seconds–minutes |
| `batch` | Job worker, streams Arrow batches | Batch iteration, part-file checkpointing, progress | minutes |
| `external` | Job worker, async pool | Concurrency limit, retry/backoff, **result cache**, cost accounting, budget cap, structured output, row-level failure isolation | minutes–hours |
| `inspect` | Request thread, synchronous | Read-only catalog + sampled query access | milliseconds |

`inspect` is the answer to "visualization is very different": visualizers and suggesters don't produce data at all — they produce a **document** (a spec, a list of suggestions). They're synchronous, cheap, and never create a job. Everything that produces data goes through the job path.

### The six kinds

```python
class Plugin(Protocol):
    id: str                    # "normalize.ip", "extract.llm_entities"
    kind: PluginKind
    mode: ExecMode
    version: str               # participates in cache keys
    title: str
    Params: type[BaseModel]    # ONE pydantic model -> request schema + OpenAPI
                               #   + auto-rendered UI form + agent tool schema
    accepts: Requirements      # e.g. semantic_types=["net.ip"], min_rows=...
    produces: Produces         # columns / semantic types / dataset kind added
```

`accepts`/`produces` is what makes plugins **discoverable**: given a dataset's column semantic types, the registry answers *"what can I do with this?"* — driving both the UI's action list and the agent's choices.

| # | Kind | Interface | Usual mode |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `Reader` | `to_relation(conn, uri, params) -> DuckDBPyRelation` | pushdown |
| 2 | `Detector` | `detect(col, stats, sample) -> list[SemanticGuess]` | inspect |
| 3 | **`Transform`** | **DatasetVersion → DatasetVersion** | any |
| 4 | `Aggregator` | `plan(inputs, params) -> QuerySpec` → new aggregate Dataset | pushdown |
| 5 | `Suggester` | `suggest(ctx) -> list[Suggestion]` | inspect |
| 6 | `Visualizer` | `spec(dataset, params) -> VizSpec` | inspect |

### `Transform` — one interface for normalize, extract, and annotate

This is the direct answer to *"is there a standard interface for normalization vs extraction?"* — **yes, they are the same kind.** Both take a dataset version and return a new version with columns added or rewritten. They differ only in `mode`, which selects which of three methods the plugin implements:

```python
# mode = "pushdown"  — normalization, regex extraction, type coercion
def sql(self, ctx: TransformCtx) -> SqlPlan:
    # returns projected/added SQL expressions; DuckDB does all the work
    return SqlPlan(add={"src_ip_canon": f"lower(trim({ctx.col('src_ip')}))"})

# mode = "batch"     — needs a Python library (geoip lookup, custom parsing)
def process(self, batch: pa.RecordBatch) -> pa.RecordBatch: ...

# mode = "external"  — LLM / network calls
async def process_rows(self, rows: list[dict], ctx: ExternalCtx) -> list[dict]: ...
```

The runner dispatches on mode; the *output contract is identical* in all three cases (a new `DatasetVersion` + a `Step` recording lineage). Everything downstream — versioning, profiling, the UI, the agent — is unaware of which mode was used.

`Aggregator` is a separate kind because it changes cardinality: it produces a **new Dataset**, not a new version of an existing one. It is expressed as a `QuerySpec` generator, which makes it `pushdown` by construction and keeps it composable with the query layer.

### Long-running jobs

Every non-`inspect` invocation becomes a `Job` containing one or more `Step`s. Dispatch by mode:

- **`pushdown`** — build one statement, `COPY (…) TO parquet` or `CREATE TABLE AS`. Progress via DuckDB's progress callback.
- **`batch`** — stream `rel.record_batch(100_000)` → plugin → writer. **Checkpoint every N batches** by flushing a part file and recording `rows_committed` on the step. Resume = skip committed parts.
- **`external`** — same streaming, plus an asyncio pool bounded by `plugin.max_concurrency`, retry with backoff, and a persistent result cache. Checkpointing matters most here: these are the runs that take an hour and cost money.

Resumability is exactly why the Parquet-lake layout is the default: each committed part is an immutable file, so resume is "read the watermark, skip those parts, continue."

**Job state:** `queued → running → (paused) → succeeded | failed | cancelled`, carrying `progress{rows_done, rows_total, pct, eta}`, `logs[]`, and for `external` also `cost{calls, tokens_in, tokens_out, usd, cache_hits}`.

`JobRunner` is a protocol. v0 impl = in-process `ThreadPoolExecutor` plus an asyncio loop for `external`, writing job/step rows to the catalog. Swappable for RQ/Celery/Temporal without touching a single plugin.

### Triggering a specific plugin — one uniform endpoint

Yes, there is a single API for this, and its uniformity is what makes it agent-usable:

```
GET  /api/plugins?kind=&applicable_to={dataset_id}
     -> [{id, kind, mode, title, params_schema, accepts, produces, cost_class}]

POST /api/operations
     { op: "import"|"transform"|"aggregate"|"join",
       plugin_id: "extract.llm_entities",
       inputs: [{dataset_id, version?}],
       params: {...},                    # validated against plugin.Params
       output: {name?, materialize: bool},
       dry_run?: true, max_cost_usd?: 5.0 }
     -> 202 {job_id, step_id}

GET  /api/jobs/{id}          # status, progress, cost, logs
GET  /api/jobs/{id}/stream   # SSE
POST /api/jobs/{id}/cancel

POST /api/inspect            # synchronous twin for inspect-mode plugins
     {plugin_id, dataset_id, params} -> the document (VizSpec / suggestions / guesses)
```

`GET /api/plugins` tells you what can go in `plugin_id`; each plugin's `params_schema` tells you what goes in `params`. **The UI's dynamic form renderer and the agent's tool schemas are both generated from that one response** — there is no second place where plugin metadata is written down.

`dry_run: true` runs the plugin over a sample (default 1000 rows) and returns sample output plus an extrapolated cost/time estimate without creating a version. This is a first-class flow, not an afterthought — it's how a non-technical user avoids launching a $40 extraction by accident.

### LLM-powered plugins

A plugin backed by Claude is just `mode="external"`. Everything hard about it is provided by the runtime so plugin authors never hand-roll it:

1. **Injected client.** Plugins receive `ctx.model` — never construct their own client. Centralizes auth/model choice/retries and lets tests inject a fake.
2. **Concurrency & batching.** Plugin declares `batch_size` and `max_concurrency`; the runner enforces them. The plugin just writes `async def process_rows(rows, ctx)`.
3. **Caching — automatic and mandatory.** Key = `sha256(plugin_id, plugin_version, params, model_id, input_payload)`, stored in a DuckDB cache table. Re-running an extraction over a superset of rows costs only the new rows. This is the single biggest thing that makes LLM plugins practical.
4. **Structured output.** The plugin declares an output Pydantic model; the runner uses `output_config={"format": …}`, so results arrive guaranteed-shaped. No parsing in plugin code.
5. **Cost accounting & budget.** Every call is attributed to the step; `max_cost_usd` aborts cleanly with partial results committed.
6. **Prompt-cache friendliness.** Stable prefix (system + few-shot) carries `cache_control`; volatile row data goes last; many rows per call.
7. **Row-level failure isolation.** A row that fails after retries yields `NULL` plus an `_error` column instead of killing the job. `stop_reason == "refusal"` is a row-level outcome, not a crash.
8. **Batch API opt-in.** A plugin may declare `supports_batch_api: true`; for large non-interactive runs the job uses `client.messages.batches` (50% cost) and polls.

**Agent-powered plugins.** A plugin whose implementation is itself a tool-using agent (e.g. a suggester that explores the data before proposing aggregates) is the same `external` mode, additionally handed `ctx.tools` — the **read-only** subset of the service layer (`run_query`, `profile_dataset`, `list_datasets`). Recursion guard: that tool set excludes `operations.create`, so a plugin can never spawn unbounded jobs. The top-level chat agent and an agent-powered plugin share one tool registry, differing only in permission scope.

API details already checked against current guidance: Python `anthropic` SDK, model `claude-opus-5`, `thinking: {type: "adaptive"}`, streaming, `@beta_tool` + `client.beta.messages.tool_runner`.

### Registration & third-party plugins

Built-ins register via decorator at import. Third-party plugins register via the `dataq.plugins` Python entry-point group, so `pip install dataq-plugin-geoip` adds capability with zero core changes.

### Frontend plugin registry (the mirror image)

Visualizers are `inspect`-mode: the backend returns a `VizSpec = {renderer, query: QuerySpec, spec: {...}, animate?: {field, steps}}`. The frontend has its **own** registry keyed on `renderer` → component (`vega-lite` → react-vega, `maplibre` → react-map-gl + deck.gl, `table` → virtualized grid). Consequence: a new chart type that reuses an existing renderer is a **backend-only** change; only a genuinely new rendering technology needs frontend code.

### v0 plugin inventory (driven by the taxi + auth-log datasets)

- Readers: `csv`, `parquet`, `ndjson`
- Detectors: `net.ip`, `geo.lat`/`geo.lng`, `geo.country_iso2`, `time.timestamp`, `identity.email`, `money.amount`, `categorical.enum` (low NDV), `identity.key` (high NDV)
- Transforms (`pushdown`): `normalize.ip`, `normalize.timestamp`, `normalize.country`, `normalize.numeric`
- Transform (`batch`): one example proving the non-SQL path
- Transform (`external`): one LLM extractor, stubbed behind the full `external` machinery so the path is real and tested with a fake client
- Aggregators: `agg.time_rollup`, `agg.topk`, `agg.frequency` (the rarity table)
- Suggesters: `suggest.viz`, `suggest.aggregate`, `suggest.join`
- Visualizers: `viz.histogram`, `viz.timeseries`, `viz.map_points`, `viz.table`

---

## Supporting layers

### Core objects (`backend/src/dataq/core/`)

| Object | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `Dataset` | Logical table. `kind`: `source`/`derived`/`aggregate`/`join`. |
| `DatasetVersion` | Immutable checkpoint: schema, row count, `StoredRef`, producing step. |
| `ColumnMeta` | `name`, `physical_type`, `semantic_type`, `confidence`, `role` (dimension/measure/time/key), `stats`. |
| `Step` | One operation: `op`, `plugin_id`, `params`, `inputs[]`, `outputs[]`, status, `rows_committed`, cost, logs. The replayable lineage DAG. |
| `Job` | Async execution of steps, with progress. |
| `SemanticType` | The metadata layer. Hierarchical (`geo.country_iso2` isa `categorical`), declares `joinable_with` + normalizer + detector. |

`SemanticType` is what makes join suggestion, viz suggestion and normalization automatic rather than hand-wired.

### Query layer (`backend/src/dataq/query/`)

`QuerySpec` — a structured IR compiled to DuckDB SQL:

```
{ source: DatasetRef | JoinRef,
  filters: [{col, op, value}], time_bucket: {col, interval}?,
  select: [{col, agg?, alias}], group_by: [], order_by: [], limit }
```

The UI filter builder, `Aggregator` plugins, `VizSpec`s, and the agent all emit `QuerySpec` — one IR, four producers. A raw-SQL escape hatch (`POST /api/query/sql`) runs on a **read-only** DuckDB connection with a statement-type check. Joins materialize as `kind=join` datasets backed by a view, so downstream steps treat them like any other dataset.

### Remaining API

```
POST /api/sources/preview            peek at a file before import
GET  /api/datasets  /{id}  /{id}/versions  /{id}/profile
POST /api/query      POST /api/query/sql
GET  /api/datasets/{id}/suggestions?kind=viz|aggregate|join
GET/POST /api/dashboards
POST /api/agent/chat                 (Stage 6)
```

Handlers contain no business logic — they resolve a plugin and call `dataq.services.*`. The agent binds `@beta_tool` functions to that **same service layer**, not to HTTP.

### Frontend (`frontend/`)

Vite + React + TS + TanStack Query + Tailwind. Types generated from OpenAPI via `openapi-typescript` (`make types`) so contracts can't drift. Pages: **Datasets** → **Dataset detail** (profile, semantic-type chips, editable, applicable-plugin actions, suggestion buttons) → **Query** → **Explore** (suggested charts) → **Dashboard**. A `<JobProgress>` component subscribes to the SSE stream and is reused everywhere. Animation is a client-side timeline scrubber over `VizSpec.animate`.

---

## Project setup (reproducible + deployable)

```
dataq/
├── .mise.toml            python 3.12, node 22   (also .nvmrc, .python-version)
├── Makefile              setup | dev | test | lint | types | build | docker
├── Dockerfile            stage 1 node build -> stage 2 uv sync --frozen + serve
├── docker-compose.yml    app + /data volume
├── .env.example          DATAQ_STORAGE, DATAQ_DATA_DIR, ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
├── backend/
│   ├── pyproject.toml    uv; fastapi, uvicorn, duckdb, pyarrow, pydantic v2,
│   │                     pydantic-settings, sqlmodel, anthropic; dev: pytest, ruff, mypy
│   ├── uv.lock
│   └── src/dataq/{core,plugins,storage,catalog,query,services,jobs,api}/
├── frontend/             package-lock.json committed
├── tests/                pytest; tiny fixtures under tests/data/
└── .github/workflows/ci.yml   ruff + mypy + pytest + npm run build
```

- **Python 3.12** deliberately, not the 3.14 on this machine — duckdb/pyarrow wheel availability is the binding constraint. `mise` fetches it.
- **Node is not installed here**; `mise install` provisions it. `make setup` = `mise install && uv sync && npm ci`.
- Dev: `make dev` → uvicorn :8000 + Vite :5173 with `/api` proxied.
- Prod: one container — FastAPI serves `/api` and the built bundle at `/`. All config via env (pydantic-settings), all state under a `/data` volume. Railway/Fly-deployable with a volume and no extra config.

---

## Build order

Each stage ends in something runnable.

| Stage | Content | Done when |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | Scaffolding: mise/uv/npm, Makefile, Dockerfile, CI, health endpoint, empty React app | `make dev` serves both; `make docker` builds |
| 1 | `core` models, SQLite catalog, `StorageBackend` + both impls, `Registry`, `SemanticType` registry | Write a relation, read it back, in both storage modes |
| 2 | `JobRunner` + Job/Step state machine + SSE; `Reader` plugins; import as a job | Import taxi + auth-log samples with live progress; cancel works |
| 3 | Profiler + `Detector` plugins; `Transform` kind with all three modes; `/api/operations` | Semantic types correct; a pushdown normalize and a batch transform both produce new versions; `external` path passes with a fake model client |
| 4 | `QuerySpec` compiler, `/api/query`, read-only SQL path | Correct rows; write attempts rejected |
| 5 | `Suggester` + `Visualizer` plugins, `/api/inspect`; frontend renderer registry | Charts + a taxi pickup map render from suggestions alone |
| 6 | `Aggregator` + join datasets | Auth-log rarity table built as an aggregate, then joined back to annotate events |
| 7 | Agent: `@beta_tool` wrappers over the service layer, `tool_runner`, chat panel; real LLM extractor plugin | "Busiest pickup zones on a Thursday night" produces a chart |

**Stage 5 is the first genuinely demoable point.** Realistically 0–5 is the "few hours"; 6 and 7 are follow-on sessions. I'll build in this order and stop wherever the time lands rather than leaving a layer half-done.

---

## Verification

- `make test` — pytest. Every plugin gets a golden test against a tiny fixture; the storage round-trip is parameterized over **both** backends.
- **Job machinery tests specifically**: kill a `batch` job mid-run and assert resume-from-watermark produces byte-identical output to an uninterrupted run; assert the `external` cache makes a second run over a superset issue calls only for new rows; assert `max_cost_usd` aborts with partial results committed. These are the parts most likely to be subtly wrong.
- **End-to-end smoke test** (`tests/test_e2e.py`, in CI): import a 1k-row taxi sample → assert `pickup_latitude`→`geo.lat`, `tpep_pickup_datetime`→`time.timestamp` → normalize → `QuerySpec` group-by → assert a map VizSpec is suggested → materialize an aggregate → join it back. Same for a synthetic auth-log fixture (`src_ip`→`net.ip`, country frequency aggregate joined back as a rarity annotation).
- `make lint` — ruff + mypy on `backend/`, `tsc --noEmit` on `frontend/`.
- **Manual**: `make dev`, import the real NYC taxi Parquet (~GB), confirm progress streams, profiling completes, map renders. Re-run with `DATAQ_STORAGE=duckdb` and confirm identical behaviour.
- **Deploy check**: `make docker && docker run -p 8000:8000 -v ./data:/data dataq` — full app on `localhost:8000` from a clean container.

Write config.py

"""Application configuration. All settings are env-overridable (prefix ``DATAQ_``)."""

from __future__ import annotations

from functools import lru_cache
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Literal

from pydantic_settings import BaseSettings, SettingsConfigDict


class Settings(BaseSettings):
    model_config = SettingsConfigDict(env_prefix="DATAQ_", env_file=".env", extra="ignore")

    # Where all state lives. A single directory so deployment is "mount one volume".
    data_dir: Path = Path("./data")

    # Which StorageBackend to use for dataset versions.
    #   parquet -> immutable Parquet parts (resumable jobs, S3-portable)  [default]
    #   duckdb  -> tables inside one .duckdb file (single-file, easy to host)
    storage: Literal["parquet", "duckdb"] = "parquet"

    # DuckDB runtime limits. Kept modest so a laptop stays responsive on 10GB files.
    duckdb_memory_limit: str = "4GB"
    duckdb_threads: int = 4

    # Job execution.
    job_workers: int = 2
    batch_rows: int = 100_000
    checkpoint_every_batches: int = 5

    # Profiling.
    profile_sample_rows: int = 10_000
    dry_run_rows: int = 1_000

    # Agent / LLM plugins.
    anthropic_api_key: str | None = None
    model: str = "claude-opus-5"

    @property
    def catalog_path(self) -> Path:
        return self.data_dir / "catalog.sqlite"

    @property
    def warehouse_path(self) -> Path:
        """The .duckdb file. Used as the warehouse in duckdb storage mode, and as
        the home for the external-plugin result cache in both modes."""
        return self.data_dir / "warehouse.duckdb"

    @property
    def lake_dir(self) -> Path:
        return self.data_dir / "lake"

    def ensure_dirs(self) -> None:
        self.data_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        self.lake_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)


@lru_cache
def get_settings() -> Settings:
    return Settings()
EOF

"""Core enums and value objects shared across every layer."""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Literal

# What a plugin consumes and produces -> determines its Python interface.
PluginKind = Literal["reader", "detector", "transform", "aggregator", "suggester", "visualizer"]

# How the runtime must execute a plugin -> determines scheduling and facilities.
#   pushdown : one DuckDB SQL statement
#   batch    : streamed Arrow batches in Python, part-file checkpointed
#   external : network/LLM calls; async pool, retries, result cache, cost accounting
#   inspect  : synchronous, read-only, returns a document (never creates a job)
ExecMode = Literal["pushdown", "batch", "external", "inspect"]

DatasetKind = Literal["source", "derived", "aggregate", "join"]

# How a column is used when suggesting queries and charts.
ColumnRole = Literal["dimension", "measure", "time", "key", "geo", "ignore"]

JobStatus = Literal["queued", "running", "paused", "succeeded", "failed", "cancelled"]

TERMINAL_JOB_STATUSES: frozenset[str] = frozenset({"succeeded", "failed", "cancelled"})

OperationOp = Literal["import", "transform", "aggregate", "join"]

# Cheap ordering hint surfaced in the UI so users know what they are about to launch.
CostClass = Literal["free", "cheap", "expensive"]
PYEOF

"""The semantic type registry -- DataQ's metadata layer.

A semantic type is what a column *means* ("this is an IPv4 address"), as opposed to
how it is stored ("VARCHAR"). Semantic types form a hierarchy, so a rule written for
``categorical`` automatically applies to ``geo.country_iso2``.

This registry is what makes join suggestion, chart suggestion and normalization
automatic rather than hand-wired: plugins declare which semantic types they accept
and produce, and the suggesters match columns across datasets by type.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field

from .types import ColumnRole


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class SemanticType:
    id: str
    title: str
    parent: str | None = None
    # Default role for a column of this type, used when suggesting charts/queries.
    role: ColumnRole = "dimension"
    # Whether matching columns of this type across datasets is a meaningful join.
    # High-cardinality identifiers and shared vocabularies are; free text is not.
    joinable: bool = False
    description: str = ""
    # Physical types this can plausibly sit on top of; used to skip detectors early.
    physical: tuple[str, ...] = field(default_factory=tuple)


class SemanticTypeRegistry:
    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self._types: dict[str, SemanticType] = {}

    def register(self, st: SemanticType) -> SemanticType:
        if st.id in self._types:
            raise ValueError(f"semantic type already registered: {st.id}")
        if st.parent is not None and st.parent not in self._types:
            raise ValueError(f"unknown parent {st.parent!r} for semantic type {st.id!r}")
        self._types[st.id] = st
        return st

    def get(self, type_id: str) -> SemanticType | None:
        return self._types.get(type_id)

    def require(self, type_id: str) -> SemanticType:
        st = self._types.get(type_id)
        if st is None:
            raise KeyError(f"unknown semantic type: {type_id}")
        return st

    def all(self) -> list[SemanticType]:
        return list(self._types.values())

    def ancestry(self, type_id: str) -> list[str]:
        """``geo.country_iso2`` -> ``['geo.country_iso2', 'categorical', 'text']``."""
        chain: list[str] = []
        cur: str | None = type_id
        seen: set[str] = set()
        while cur is not None and cur not in seen:
            seen.add(cur)
            chain.append(cur)
            st = self._types.get(cur)
            cur = st.parent if st else None
        return chain

    def is_a(self, type_id: str, ancestor: str) -> bool:
        return ancestor in self.ancestry(type_id)

    def matches_any(self, type_id: str | None, accepted: tuple[str, ...]) -> bool:
        """True if ``type_id`` is, or descends from, any of ``accepted``.

        An empty ``accepted`` means "no constraint".
        """
        if not accepted:
            return True
        if type_id is None:
            return False
        return any(self.is_a(type_id, a) for a in accepted)

    def joinable_with(self, type_id: str | None) -> bool:
        if type_id is None:
            return False
        st = self._types.get(type_id)
        return bool(st and st.joinable)


SEMANTIC_TYPES = SemanticTypeRegistry()


def _r(*args, **kwargs) -> SemanticType:
    return SEMANTIC_TYPES.register(SemanticType(*args, **kwargs))


# --- roots -------------------------------------------------------------------
_r("numeric", "Number", role="measure", physical=("BIGINT", "INTEGER", "DOUBLE", "DECIMAL"))
_r("text", "Text", role="dimension", physical=("VARCHAR",))
_r("temporal", "Date/Time", role="time", physical=("TIMESTAMP", "DATE", "TIME"))
_r("boolean", "Boolean", role="dimension", physical=("BOOLEAN",))

# A low-cardinality value set. Joinable because a shared vocabulary (status codes,
# categories, country names) is exactly what you join two datasets on.
_r("categorical", "Category", parent="text", role="dimension", joinable=True)

# --- geo ---------------------------------------------------------------------
_r("geo.lat", "Latitude", parent="numeric", role="geo",
   description="Decimal degrees, -90..90")
_r("geo.lng", "Longitude", parent="numeric", role="geo",
   description="Decimal degrees, -180..180")
_r("geo.country_iso2", "Country (ISO-3166 alpha-2)", parent="categorical", role="dimension",
   joinable=True, description="Two-letter uppercase country code")

# --- time --------------------------------------------------------------------

_r("time.date", "Date", parent="temporal", role="time")

# --- network -----------------------------------------------------------------

   description="IPv4 or IPv6 address")
